Verified against build_paged_url_impl: the OffsetLimit arm appends offset=N&limit=M for non-POST and injects offset/limit as top-level JSON body keys for POST; the only other arm CursorToken emits cursor and page_size. No pagination type in the engine grammar emits page/size. AC-003 and AC-004 are jointly unsatisfiable. Consequence if shipped: prism POSTs {offset:0,limit:100} to an API reading ?page=&size=; parameters are ignored; page 1 returns on every iteration; build_paged_url termination condition exits after one page — silent truncation of a security alerts feed (CWE-390 class). NEW LATENT DEFECT — GAP-ASSETS-PAG-001: Cyberint assets endpoint has server-controlled page size. GetAssetsRequest declares page_number (integer, minimum 1, default 1) inline with no page_size field and no reference to the Pagination-Input schema (that schema belongs to GetAssetsTechnologiesRequest — a different endpoint). ADR-056 ratified PageNumber { page_size } variant cannot cover it: termination rule page_record_count < page_size requires a client-known page_size that this endpoint does not expose. Without pagination block, prism sends no page_number, server applies default page 1, all assets beyond page 1 are silently dropped — CWE-390 silent-truncation class, same category as CRIT-003. GetAssetsResponse.total_assets is the evidence field. Alerts surface unaffected (GetAlertsRequest has client-specifiable size with maximum 100; uses ratified page_number variant). Implementing engineer required by T-03 to leave a GAP-ASSETS-PAG-001 comment in TOML. (2) FB53a: orchestrator asserted CursorPagination was referenced by no endpoint (false; it is reached via $ref from GetCredentialsRequest.properties.pagination; scan searched only serialized operation objects and missed $ref indirection). | DRIFT-D904-001 [process-gap, JUSTIFIED DEFERRAL; D-904] | OBS-PR1-001 adversary diff-tooling limitation: the vsdd-factory adversary agent receives PR diffs via `gh pr diff` CLI output but lacks the ability to interactively navigate to specific line contexts within large diffs. The dispatch tuple (worktree-abs-path, feature-HEAD-SHA, story-id, canonical-repo-root) partially mitigates this by ensuring the adversary can read the feature worktree's full files, but it does not eliminate the diff-navigation limitation for very large diffs. Tracked in drbothen/vsdd-factory upstream issue tracker. | Track in drbothen/vsdd-factory upstream | upstream |
| DRIFT-D904-002 [process-gap, JUSTIFIED DEFERRAL; D-904] | OBS-PR2 worktree-path-resolution hazard: agents dispatched to read feature-branch code without an explicit worktree path in their dispatch tuple may accidentally read from the develop checkout rather than the feature worktree. The recommended workaround — codified as DRIFT-ORCH-ADVERSARY-TUPLE-001 and DRIFT-ORCH-AGENT-WORKTREE-READ-001 — is for the orchestrator to always embed the absolute worktree path, feature HEAD SHA, and story-id in every feature-code-reading dispatch. | DRIFT-D923-002 [architect/PO-scope; D-923] | `validate_sensor_spec()` in prism-spec-engine performs syntactic and semantic validation of a loaded TOML sensor spec but is only called from test code (unit tests under `crates/prism-spec-engine/tests/`). The production spec-load path (`SpecEngine::load_sensor_spec()`) calls `parse_sensor_spec()` without calling `validate_sensor_spec()` afterward. This means a malformed or semantically-invalid TOML sensor spec (e.g., a table with duplicate column names, an invalid auth_type value, a negative timeout) can be loaded at runtime without triggering validation errors that would surface to the operator. The production impact depends on whether downstream code handles invalid spec state gracefully. Architect/PO adjudication needed: should `validate_sensor_spec()` be called inline in `load_sensor_spec()`, or should validation be a separate pre-load gate called by the orchestration layer? | Architect/PO adjudicate | separate disposition |

| GAP-ASSETS-PAG-001 [new-latent-defect; D-2030/FB53c; owner: architect+orchestrator; status: OPEN — orchestrator-flagged, NOT yet authorized by human] | Cyberint assets endpoint (`POST /external/api/v1/assets/`) has server-controlled page size. `GetAssetsRequest` declares `page_number` (integer, minimum 1, default 1) inline with no `page_size` field and no reference to the `Pagination-Input` schema — that schema belongs to `GetAssetsTechnologiesRequest`, a different endpoint. ADR-056 ratified `PageNumber { page_size: u32 }` variant cannot cover it: the termination rule `page_record_count < page_size` requires a client-known page size that this endpoint does not expose. Without a `[tables.steps.pagination]` block, prism sends no `page_number`, the server applies default page 1, and all assets beyond page 1 are silently dropped — CWE-390 silent-truncation class, same category as CRIT-003 on alerts. `GetAssetsResponse.total_assets` is the evidence field: if `total_assets > len(assets)` on the first response, later assets were missed. Alerts surface unaffected (`GetAlertsRequest` has client-specifiable `size` with maximum 100, uses ADR-056 ratified `page_number` variant). Implementing engineer required by T-03 in S-WAVE-A-CYBERINT-SPEC-001 to leave a `# GAP-ASSETS-PAG-001` comment in cyberint-assets.sensor.toml marking the gap as traceable at code level. | Architect: design a new `PaginationConfig` variant for server-controlled page size — termination driven by `total_assets` comparison or short/empty page detection rather than client-known `page_size`. Same blast-radius as ADR-056: new enum variant, three `pipeline.rs` dispatch sites (`build_paged_url_impl`, `build_request`, `execute_impl`), BC-2.16.002 postcondition row, new ADR. Follow-up story TBD — do NOT create story without explicit human authorization. | human authorization pending |
